- **Abstract**: Facial micro-expressions (MEs) are involuntary movements of the face that occur spontaneously when a person experiences an emotion but attempts to suppress or repress the facial expression, typically found in a high-stakes environment. In recent years, substantial advancements have been made in the areas of ME recognition, spotting, and generation. However, conventional approaches that treat spotting and recognition as separate tasks are suboptimal, particularly for analyzing long-duration videos in realistic settings. Concurrently, the emergence of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) and large vision-language models (LVLMs) offers promising new avenues for enhancing ME analysis through their powerful multimodal reasoning capabilities. The ME grand challenge (MEGC) 2025 introduces two tasks that reflect these evolving research directions: (1) ME spot-then-recognize (ME-STR), which integrates ME spotting and subsequent recognition in a unified sequential pipeline; and (2) ME visual question answering (ME-VQA), which explores ME understanding through visual question answering, leveraging MLLMs or LVLMs to address diverse question types related to MEs. All participating algorithms are required to run on this test set and submit their results on a leaderboard. More details are available at https://megc2025.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the MEGC2025 (Micro-Expression Grand Challenge 2025), focusing on two tasks: ME Spot-Then-Recognize (ME-STR) and ME Visual Question Answering (ME-VQA). The ME-STR task integrates ME spotting and recognition into a single pipeline, aiming to address limitations where spotting and recognition are treated as separate tasks. The ME-VQA task explores understanding MEs using visual question answering, leveraging multimodal large language models (MLLMs) or large vision-language models (LVLMs).  The paper describes the datasets used, the methods, and the evaluation metrics employed for both tasks.  Baseline results using MEAN for ME-STR and Qwen2.5VL-3B for ME-VQA are presented.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The ME-STR task builds upon previous challenges by continuing to explore the spot-then-recognize paradigm, attempting to integrate these two steps into one.  The ME-VQA task is a *novel* approach, introducing a multimodal aspect to ME analysis by reformulating annotations into question-answer pairs, leveraging recent advances in MLLMs/LVLMs. This is the more significant of the two tasks in terms of novelty. Moving away from discrete emotion classification, is also something that the field could benefit from.

*   **Significance:**  The ME-STR task addresses a real-world limitation in ME analysis, where MEs are rarely isolated, and recognition often relies on pre-spotted data. By integrating spotting and recognition, the challenge aims to push the field towards more practical applications. The ME-VQA task has the potential to significantly broaden the scope of ME research by enabling more complex and nuanced inquiries about facial expressions, fostering interpretability and human-aligned interactions through natural language. It can facilitate research that goes beyond simple emotion classification, incorporating contextual understanding and enabling reasoning about MEs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The introduction of the ME-VQA task represents a significant shift in how ME understanding is approached, moving towards a multimodal, interactive paradigm.
    *   The challenge provides a standardized platform and datasets for comparing and evaluating different approaches.
    *   By building upon past MEGCs, it fosters continuous progress and encourages researchers to address the challenges of previous iterations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The baseline results for ME-STR (particularly low F1-scores and recall in the spotting stage) highlight the difficulty of the task and the need for further improvements in spotting algorithms. The ME-STR paradigm might be more useful for understanding expressions.
    *   The paper provides relatively basic baseline results for ME-VQA. The performance metrics (BLEU and ROUGE) may not perfectly capture the nuances of emotion understanding. These metrics tend to favor literal matches rather than semantic accuracy.
    *   The datasets available to construct a ME-VQA training set are not large.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The ME-VQA task has the potential to encourage the development of more sophisticated ME analysis techniques that integrate visual information with natural language understanding. It also has the potential to foster new applications of ME analysis in areas such as human-computer interaction, mental health assessment, and security. The emphasis on spot-then-recognize is useful as the goal is to automate ME assessment.

*   **Justification for the Score:** While ME-STR is a continuation of an existing paradigm, the introduction of the ME-VQA task with the incorporation of MLLMs/LVLMs marks a significant step forward. This new task has the potential to reshape the field by enabling more nuanced and interpretable analysis of micro-expressions. However, the limited baseline results and relatively small scale of the test dataset tempers my enthusiasm somewhat. Moreover, the reliance on BLEU and ROUGE for evaluating ME-VQA introduces a potential weakness as they may not fully capture the semantic accuracy of the answers. The paper overall offers a promising vision for the future of ME research, but more data is needed, and more powerful methods need to be developed.

### SecFwT: Efficient Privacy-Preserving Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models Using Forward-Only Passes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15307v1)
- **Authors**: Jinglong Luo, Zhuo Zhang, Yehong Zhang, Shiyu Liu, Ye Dong, Xun Zhou, Hui Wang, Yue Yu, Zenglin Xu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have transformed numerous fields, yet their adaptation to specialized tasks in privacy-sensitive domains, such as healthcare and finance, is constrained by the scarcity of accessible training data due to stringent privacy requirements. Secure multi-party computation (MPC)-based privacy-preserving machine learning offers a powerful approach to protect both model parameters and user data, but its application to LLMs has been largely limited to inference, as fine-tuning introduces significant computational challenges, particularly in privacy-preserving backward propagation and optimizer operations. This paper identifies two primary obstacles to MPC-based privacy-preserving fine-tuning of LLMs: (1) the substantial computational overhead of backward and optimizer processes, and (2) the inefficiency of softmax-based attention mechanisms in MPC settings. To address these challenges, we propose SecFwT, the first MPC-based framework designed for efficient, privacy-preserving LLM fine-tuning. SecFwT introduces a forward-only tuning paradigm to eliminate backward and optimizer computations and employs MPC-friendly Random Feature Attention to approximate softmax attention, significantly reducing costly non-linear operations and computational complexity. Experimental results demonstrate that SecFwT delivers substantial improvements in efficiency and privacy preservation, enabling scalable and secure fine-tuning of LLMs for privacy-critical applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SECFWT: Efficient Privacy-Preserving Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models Using Forward-Only Passes" addresses the challenge of privacy-preserving fine-tuning (PPFT) of large language models (LLMs) using Secure Multi-Party Computation (MPC).  The key idea is to avoid computationally expensive backward propagation and optimizer operations by using a forward-only tuning paradigm (FoT) based on gradient-free optimization (GFO), combined with an MPC-friendly approximation of softmax attention using Random Feature Attention (RFA). They propose several optimizations within the MPC context to improve efficiency. Experiments show significant speedups and reduced communication costs compared to traditional PPFT methods, with only a small performance degradation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in combining forward-only fine-tuning and random feature attention specifically within an MPC framework for LLMs. While FoT and RFA exist independently, their adaptation and optimization for MPC, particularly in the context of LLMs, is a significant contribution. Addressing the computational bottlenecks in backward propagation and optimizing softmax for secure computation are novel and practical concerns. The paper also provides tailored optimizations for RFA within the MPC setting. The interaction paradigm to leverage encrypted communication between servers and data owners for loss evaluation and derivative-free optimization is also innovative.
*   **Significance:** The research addresses a crucial problem limiting the adoption of LLMs in privacy-sensitive domains: the computational burden of secure fine-tuning. Enabling efficient PPFT is essential for unlocking the potential of LLMs in healthcare, finance, and other sectors where data privacy is paramount. By substantially reducing the computational and communication overhead, the proposed approach makes PPFT more practical and scalable. The results demonstrate that the approach has practical benefits, leading to a significant reduction in time and communication costs for PPFT. While the performance degradation on more challenging classification tasks is a concern, the authors suggest mitigation strategies, which is a positive point.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained technical approach with detailed algorithms and optimizations.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating substantial efficiency gains.
    *   Comprehensive comparison with relevant baselines.
    *   Addresses a significant challenge in the field of privacy-preserving machine learning.
    *   Practical and scalable solution suitable for real-world applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance degradation on more complex tasks like AG's News is a noticeable limitation, even if mitigatable with more iterations. A more in-depth analysis of this degradation and its potential solutions could be helpful.
    *   The reliance on the ROBERTALARGE architecture.  While reasonable given prior work, demonstrating broader applicability to other LLM architectures would strengthen the paper.
    *   Limited evaluation of various GFO methods. The impact of different GFO algorithms on performance and security in this context could be explored.
    *   The paper could provide more discussion on the security implications of the choice of random features and GFO used. How do the security guarantees from the underlying MPC framework and the GFO methods interplay?

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by enabling more practical and scalable privacy-preserving fine-tuning of LLMs. This could lead to increased adoption of LLMs in privacy-sensitive applications and foster collaborative research in this area. The approach provides a solid foundation for future research, exploring more sophisticated optimization techniques, and generalizing to other LLM architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty in adapting FoT and RFA to an LLM fine-tuning MPC setting, the well-executed experimental evaluation, and the practical significance of the work in enabling PPFT of LLMs, but also considering the weaknesses listed above, a score of 7 is justified.  It represents a significant and practical contribution to the field, addressing a key challenge. The limitations, mainly the performance degradation on complex tasks and focus on a specific LLM architecture, prevent a higher score, as there's room for improvement and broader applicability.

### One-shot Face Sketch Synthesis in the Wild via Generative Diffusion Prior and Instruction Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15312v1)
- **Authors**: Han Wu, Junyao Li, Kangbo Zhao, Sen Zhang, Yukai Shi, Liang Lin
- **Abstract**: Face sketch synthesis is a technique aimed at converting face photos into sketches. Existing face sketch synthesis research mainly relies on training with numerous photo-sketch sample pairs from existing datasets. However, these large-scale discriminative learning methods will have to face problems such as data scarcity and high human labor costs. Once the training data becomes scarce, their generative performance significantly degrades. In this paper, we propose a one-shot face sketch synthesis method based on diffusion models. We optimize text instructions on a diffusion model using face photo-sketch image pairs. Then, the instructions derived through gradient-based optimization are used for inference. To simulate real-world scenarios more accurately and evaluate method effectiveness more comprehensively, we introduce a new benchmark named One-shot Face Sketch Dataset (OS-Sketch). The benchmark consists of 400 pairs of face photo-sketch images, including sketches with different styles and photos with different backgrounds, ages, sexes, expressions, illumination, etc. For a solid out-of-distribution evaluation, we select only one pair of images for training at each time, with the rest used for inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed method can convert various photos into realistic and highly consistent sketches in a one-shot context. Compared to other methods, our approach offers greater convenience and broader applicability. The dataset will be available at: https://github.com/HanWu3125/OS-Sketch
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel one-shot face sketch synthesis method using a generative diffusion prior and instruction tuning. The core idea is to optimize text instructions for a diffusion model using only *one* face photo-sketch image pair for training. These optimized instructions are then used to convert various unseen face photos into realistic and consistent sketches. To address the limitations of existing datasets, the authors also introduce a new benchmark dataset, OS-Sketch, containing diverse sketch styles and photos with varying backgrounds, ages, sexes, expressions, and illumination conditions. The paper demonstrates through extensive experiments that the proposed method achieves promising results in a one-shot setting, offering greater convenience and broader applicability compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach. While diffusion models and instruction tuning have been explored in image editing, applying them to the specific problem of one-shot face sketch synthesis is a unique contribution. Optimizing text instructions based on a single image pair to guide the diffusion model is a clever way to overcome the data scarcity issue in sketch synthesis. The introduction of a new, more diverse dataset (OS-Sketch) also adds to the novelty.  However, visual prompt-based image editing, as cited, is a related area, and the extension to sketch synthesis isn't a groundbreaking conceptual leap. The innovation lies in the specific implementation and the empirical results.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from addressing a critical challenge in face sketch synthesis: the reliance on large, labeled datasets. The one-shot approach significantly reduces data and labeling costs, making the technique more practical for real-world applications. The OS-Sketch dataset provides a valuable resource for evaluating one-shot sketch synthesis methods and is a necessary addition given the limitations of existing datasets.  The improved performance compared to other methods in the one-shot setting demonstrates the potential of the proposed framework. The in-the-wild experiments show that it moves towards practical usage. However, the method still relies on a pre-trained diffusion model and some automated prompt generation, limiting the "one-shot" claim's purity.  It would have been stronger if they could demonstrate the generation of diverse sketches from scratch without any prior training on large datasets.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a practical limitation of existing methods.
    *   Introduces a novel application of diffusion models.
    *   Presents a new, challenging, and more diverse dataset.
    *   Demonstrates strong performance in a one-shot setting.
    *   Well-written and clearly explains the methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Relies on a pre-trained diffusion model, somewhat diluting the one-shot nature.
    *   The text instruction optimization might be sensitive to the initial prompt.
    *   Limited exploration of different diffusion architectures and their impact.
    *   The qualitative results, while promising, still show some imperfections.
    * The dataset is small (400 images). Ideally, it should be much larger.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in face sketch synthesis by promoting the development of data-efficient methods. The OS-Sketch dataset can serve as a benchmark for evaluating new approaches.  The use of instruction tuning with diffusion models could be adopted for other image-to-image translation tasks where data is scarce.

* **Further Discussion:** The paper could benefit from further analysis of the limitations, particularly the dependence on a pre-trained diffusion model. A more comprehensive ablation study exploring the influence of different diffusion model parameters and prompt initialization strategies would also be valuable. In the future, the method could explore user interaction methods for adjusting sketch generation.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of face sketch synthesis by addressing the data scarcity problem with a one-shot learning approach. The introduction of the OS-Sketch dataset is a valuable addition. While it builds upon existing diffusion models and instruction tuning techniques, its clever adaptation and the demonstrated empirical results justify a reasonably high score. However, reliance on pre-trained models and potential sensitivities in text optimization prevent it from achieving an even higher score. It offers a practical and promising avenue for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### When and How Unlabeled Data Provably Improve In-Context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15329v1)
- **Authors**: Yingcong Li, Xiangyu Chang, Muti Kara, Xiaofeng Liu, Amit Roy-Chowdhury, Samet Oymak
- **Abstract**: Recent research shows that in-context learning (ICL) can be effective even when demonstrations have missing or incorrect labels. To shed light on this capability, we examine a canonical setting where the demonstrations are drawn according to a binary Gaussian mixture model (GMM) and a certain fraction of the demonstrations have missing labels. We provide a comprehensive theoretical study to show that: (1) The loss landscape of one-layer linear attention models recover the optimal fully-supervised estimator but completely fail to exploit unlabeled data; (2) In contrast, multilayer or looped transformers can effectively leverage unlabeled data by implicitly constructing estimators of the form $\sum_{i\ge 0} a_i (X^\top X)^iX^\top y$ with $X$ and $y$ denoting features and partially-observed labels (with missing entries set to zero). We characterize the class of polynomials that can be expressed as a function of depth and draw connections to Expectation Maximization, an iterative pseudo-labeling algorithm commonly used in semi-supervised learning. Importantly, the leading polynomial power is exponential in depth, so mild amount of depth/looping suffices. As an application of theory, we propose looping off-the-shelf tabular foundation models to enhance their semi-supervision capabilities. Extensive evaluations on real-world datasets show that our method significantly improves the semisupervised tabular learning performance over the standard single pass inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how unlabeled data can improve in-context learning (ICL) in transformers, focusing on a semi-supervised setting with Gaussian Mixture Models (GMMs). The authors theoretically demonstrate that single-layer linear attention models fail to leverage unlabeled data, while multi-layer (or looped) transformers can effectively use unlabeled data by implicitly constructing estimators similar to those used in Expectation Maximization (EM). They show that logarithmic depth is sufficient and propose a looping strategy for tabular foundation models to enhance semi-supervision. Empirical evaluations on real-world datasets validate the performance improvements with the proposed method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical Rigor:** The paper provides a solid theoretical framework, analyzing the loss landscape of linear attention and characterizing the expressive power of deeper transformers in the context of semi-supervised ICL. This is a valuable contribution, offering insights into why depth is crucial for leveraging unlabeled data.
    *   **Connection to EM:** The explicit connection made between multi-layer attention and EM, demonstrating how the model implicitly performs pseudo-labeling and maximization steps, is insightful and provides a novel perspective.
    *   **Practical Application:** The looping strategy for tabular foundation models offers a practical approach to improve semi-supervised learning performance on real-world datasets. The empirical results support the theoretical findings and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    *   **Clear Research Question:** The paper tackles a relevant question regarding the utilization of weak supervision in the context of the increasing size of demonstrations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Architecture:** The core theoretical analysis is primarily focused on *linear attention* models, a specific (and arguably simpler) type of attention mechanism. While the authors extend the discussion to softmax attention and transformers empirically, the theoretical guarantees do not directly apply to these more complex architectures. The generalization to other architectures relies heavily on the EM-analogy.
    *   **Specific Data Distribution:** The GMM assumption, while a common starting point for theoretical analysis, might limit the generality of the results. The effectiveness of the proposed method in scenarios with more complex or non-parametric data distributions may vary.
    *   **Incremental Novelty in Application:** The LoopTabFM algorithm, while showing performance improvements, relies heavily on TabPFN. The core novelty lies in the *application* of an iterative pseudo-labeling strategy (which is already well established in semi-supervised learning) within the ICL context and to tabular data. The practical contribution is therefore, smaller.
    *   **Lack of Depth in Dataset Exploration:**  Although the paper includes tabular experiments, the analysis of dataset-specific properties or characteristics (like noise levels, data balance etc.) would provide additional insights and robustness.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The theoretical analysis of semi-supervised ICL, characterizing the role of depth in linear attention models, is a novel contribution.
    *   The connection between multi-layer attention and EM provides a valuable interpretation of how transformers can leverage unlabeled data.
    *   The looping strategy and its application to tabular foundation models are a practical and interesting extension of the theory.
    *   However, the reliance on linear attention in the theoretical core and TabPFN in the empirical application moderately limits the overall impact.
    * The presented analysis provides insights that are valuable for designing/interpreting transformer architectures.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a valuable theoretical analysis of semi-supervised ICL, particularly in the context of linear attention models. The connection to EM and the proposed looping strategy provide interesting perspectives and practical benefits. However, the specific focus on linear attention and the incremental nature of the empirical application limit the overall scope and significance.

### DeVisE: Behavioral Testing of Medical Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15339v1)
- **Authors**: Camila Zurdo Tagliabue, Heloisa Oss Boll, Aykut Erdem, Erkut Erdem, Iacer Calixto
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in clinical decision support, yet current evaluation methods often fail to distinguish genuine medical reasoning from superficial patterns. We introduce DeVisE (Demographics and Vital signs Evaluation), a behavioral testing framework for probing fine-grained clinical understanding. We construct a dataset of ICU discharge notes from MIMIC-IV, generating both raw (real-world) and template-based (synthetic) versions with controlled single-variable counterfactuals targeting demographic (age, gender, ethnicity) and vital sign attributes. We evaluate five LLMs spanning general-purpose and medically fine-tuned variants, under both zero-shot and fine-tuned settings. We assess model behavior via (1) input-level sensitivity - how counterfactuals alter the likelihood of a note; and (2) downstream reasoning - how they affect predicted hospital length-of-stay. Our results show that zero-shot models exhibit more coherent counterfactual reasoning patterns, while fine-tuned models tend to be more stable yet less responsive to clinically meaningful changes. Notably, demographic factors subtly but consistently influence outputs, emphasizing the importance of fairness-aware evaluation. This work highlights the utility of behavioral testing in exposing the reasoning strategies of clinical LLMs and informing the design of safer, more transparent medical AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DeVisE: Behavioral Testing of Medical Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeVisE, a behavioral testing framework for evaluating the fine-grained clinical understanding of Large Language Models (LLMs). The framework uses minimally differing counterfactuals targeting demographic attributes (age, gender, ethnicity) and vital signs (heart rate, respiration rate, oxygen saturation, blood pressure, and temperature) in ICU discharge summaries from MIMIC-IV. The authors generate both raw (real-world) and template-based (synthetic) versions of clinical notes and evaluate the LLMs on sensitivity to input changes and downstream effects on predicted hospital length-of-stay. They assess various LLMs (general-purpose and medically fine-tuned, zero-shot and fine-tuned) to uncover reasoning strategies and inform the design of safer, more transparent medical AI systems. The key findings suggest that zero-shot models exhibit more coherent counterfactual reasoning, while fine-tuned models are more stable but less responsive to clinically meaningful changes. Demographic factors subtly influence outputs, emphasizing the importance of fairness-aware evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of behavioral testing to medical LLMs using a dataset of both raw and template-based clinical notes with controlled counterfactuals.  While behavioral testing and counterfactual analysis are not new concepts *per se*, their systematic and comprehensive application to clinical LLMs, particularly with the inclusion of both demographic and vital sign attributes and raw clinical text rather than solely structured data, represents a significant contribution. Previous work has primarily focused on structured data or synthetic inputs, whereas this study offers a more realistic and nuanced approach. This is also one of the first behavioral testing benchmark for medical LLMs released, thus filling an existing gap.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need in the evaluation of medical LLMs: the limitations of traditional benchmarks that often fail to distinguish genuine medical reasoning from superficial pattern matching. DeVisE offers a more granular and interpretable way to assess model behavior, highlighting potential biases and weaknesses in reasoning strategies. The framework's ability to expose subtle influences of demographic factors is particularly significant from a fairness and ethical perspective.  The findings on the differing behaviors of zero-shot and fine-tuned models are also valuable, suggesting that fine-tuning may not always lead to improved robustness or sensitivity to clinically relevant information.  The comparison between template-based and raw notes is also important, as it reveals that lack of context can be an important limiting factor in fine-tuning LLMs.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive framework:** DeVisE provides a well-defined and systematic methodology for behavioral testing of medical LLMs.
    * **Realistic dataset:** The use of both raw and template-based clinical notes from MIMIC-IV enhances the realism and applicability of the findings.
    * **Meaningful counterfactuals:** The counterfactuals are based on clinically relevant variables and guidelines, ensuring that the tests are grounded in medical knowledge.
    * **Thorough analysis:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of model behavior, using a variety of metrics and comparisons.
    * **Focus on Fairness and Bias:** Explicitly focusing on identifying biases towards certain groups is an important contribution.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Generalizability:** The dataset is derived from a single hospital in the US, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other healthcare settings.
    * **Simplified Task:** While LOS is clinically relevant, it is a relatively simplified downstream task, that can be potentially influenced by many more factors than those explicitly modified within the context of the counterfactuals, that are not explicitly considered in the model.
    * **Limited Model Coverage:** While five LLMs were evaluated, the field of LLMs is rapidly evolving, and future work should include more models and architectures.
    * **Potential limitations of LOS selection:** LOS is a complex outcome influenced by numerous factors beyond admission data. Although the authors acknowledge this, using a more proximal outcome or a task designed to explicitly measure reasoning over the manipulated variables could strengthen the conclusions.

* **Potential Influence:** DeVisE has the potential to influence the development and evaluation of medical LLMs by providing a more rigorous and interpretable assessment framework. It could also inform the design of training strategies that improve model robustness, fairness, and sensitivity to clinically relevant information.  The benchmark is released as open-source code, allowing for broader adoption.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

DeVisE represents a significant contribution to the field of medical LLM evaluation by providing a practical and insightful behavioral testing framework. The paper addresses a clear need for more granular and interpretable assessment methods, and its findings have important implications for the development of safer and more reliable medical AI systems. The inclusion of raw clinical notes, focus on fairness and biases and comparisons between different LLM architectures is an important step in the right direction.  While limitations related to generalizability and the relatively simplified downstream task exist, the overall impact and potential influence of DeVisE warrant a score of 8. This score reflects the paper's solid methodology, significant findings, and potential to shape future research in this critical area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Acoustic Waveform Inversion with Image-to-Image Schrödinger Bridges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15346v1)
- **Authors**: A. S. Stankevich, I. B. Petrov
- **Abstract**: Recent developments in application of deep learning models to acoustic Full Waveform Inversion (FWI) are marked by the use of diffusion models as prior distributions for Bayesian-like inference procedures. The advantage of these methods is the ability to generate high-resolution samples, which are otherwise unattainable with classical inversion methods or other deep learning-based solutions. However, the iterative and stochastic nature of sampling from diffusion models along with heuristic nature of output control remain limiting factors for their applicability. For instance, an optimal way to include the approximate velocity model into diffusion-based inversion scheme remains unclear, even though it is considered an essential part of FWI pipeline. We address the issue by employing a Schr\"odinger Bridge that interpolates between the distributions of ground truth and smoothed velocity models. To facilitate the learning of nonlinear drifts that transfer samples between distributions we extend the concept of Image-to-Image Schr\"odinger Bridge ($\text{I}^2\text{SB}$) to conditional sampling, resulting in a conditional Image-to-Image Schr\"odinger Bridge (c$\text{I}^2\text{SB}$) framework. To validate our method, we assess its effectiveness in reconstructing the reference velocity model from its smoothed approximation, coupled with the observed seismic signal of fixed shape. Our experiments demonstrate that the proposed solution outperforms our reimplementation of conditional diffusion model suggested in earlier works, while requiring only a few neural function evaluations (NFEs) to achieve sample fidelity superior to that attained with supervised learning-based approach. The supplementary code implementing the algorithms described in this paper can be found in the repository https://github.com/stankevich-mipt/seismic_inversion_via_I2SB.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to acoustic Full Waveform Inversion (FWI) using a conditional Image-to-Image Schrödinger Bridge (cI2SB). This method aims to improve upon existing diffusion-based FWI techniques by incorporating smoothed velocity models more theoretically and efficiently. The cI2SB framework interpolates between the distributions of ground truth and smoothed velocity models, guiding the inference process from an approximate model to a high-resolution sample in a finite time. The authors extend the Image-to-Image Schrödinger Bridge (I2SB) concept to conditional sampling, resulting in the cI2SB framework. They validate the method's effectiveness in reconstructing reference velocity models from smoothed approximations, conditioned on seismic signals. Experiments demonstrate that the proposed solution outperforms a reimplementation of conditional diffusion models from earlier works and achieves superior sample fidelity with fewer neural function evaluations (NFEs) compared to supervised learning approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents some degree of novelty by employing Schrödinger Bridges (SB) for FWI and adapting the I2SB framework for conditional seismic inversion. The use of SB, particularly with Image-to-Image translations, is a less explored avenue than standard diffusion in this domain. The extension of I2SB to a *conditional* setting (cI2SB) to handle seismic data seems to be a genuine contribution. The training regime involving a blend of conditional and unconditional training also marks a contribution, though related methods exist.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the limitations of existing diffusion-based FWI methods, specifically the lack of theoretical justification for incorporating approximate velocity models and the iterative and stochastic nature of diffusion sampling. The cI2SB approach offers a more controlled and efficient inference process, potentially reducing computational costs and improving the accuracy of velocity model reconstruction. Further, the paper shows that better results can be achieved by having better theoretical underpinnings as compared to previous *ad-hoc* methods in deep learning based FWI.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical Soundness:**  The method builds upon the well-established framework of Schrödinger Bridges and provides a more theoretically grounded approach for incorporating prior information compared to purely heuristic conditioning methods in diffusion models. The amortized conditional SB method with a classifier-free guidance analogue is well-motivated.

    *   **Efficiency:** The paper claims and demonstrates improved sample fidelity with fewer NFEs compared to supervised or other diffusion-based methods, which can lead to significant computational savings. The method outperforms several previously-proposed methods.

    *   **Experimental Validation:** The use of the OpenFWI dataset provides a standardized benchmark for evaluation and allows for comparison with existing methods. Experiments demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms reimplemented conditional diffusion models and achieves sample fidelity superior to supervised learning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Real-World Validation:** The experiments are conducted on synthetic data from the OpenFWI dataset. While this is a common practice, the performance of the method on real seismic data remains to be evaluated. The authors do recognize that the transferability between different basins could be limited.

    *   **Dependency on Distortion Operator:**  The reliance on an explicitly designed distortion operator *D* is a limitation, as performance can be affected if the inference distribution of initial guesses differs from what the model was trained on. The authors do a limited study on this limitation and note the lack of robustness is an area for improvement.

    *   **Black Box Deep Learning:** As with many deep learning methods, the interpretability of the learned nonlinear drifts and the exact mechanisms that lead to improved performance remain somewhat unclear. Although they do relate bias amplification to the tradeoff in perception and distortion.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the proposed method proves to be robust and efficient in real-world scenarios, it could have a significant impact on the field of seismic inversion. The reduced computational cost and improved accuracy could enable more detailed subsurface imaging and improved exploration and monitoring of subsurface resources. Further work building on the SB based inversion is expected as the authors have shown solid theoretical footing as compared to heuristic methods.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a novel application of Schrödinger Bridges to acoustic FWI, addressing the limitations of existing diffusion-based methods. The improved efficiency and demonstrated performance gains on synthetic data warrant a positive evaluation. However, the lack of real-world validation and reliance on the distortion operator limit the current significance. The method is well-motivated and presented but not necessarily game-changing to the level required for a score of 8 or above.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### SANSKRITI: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Evaluating Language Models' Knowledge of Indian Culture
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15355v1)
- **Authors**: Arijit Maji, Raghvendra Kumar, Akash Ghosh, Anushka, Sriparna Saha
- **Abstract**: Language Models (LMs) are indispensable tools shaping modern workflows, but their global effectiveness depends on understanding local socio-cultural contexts. To address this, we introduce SANSKRITI, a benchmark designed to evaluate language models' comprehension of India's rich cultural diversity. Comprising 21,853 meticulously curated question-answer pairs spanning 28 states and 8 union territories, SANSKRITI is the largest dataset for testing Indian cultural knowledge. It covers sixteen key attributes of Indian culture: rituals and ceremonies, history, tourism, cuisine, dance and music, costume, language, art, festivals, religion, medicine, transport, sports, nightlife, and personalities, providing a comprehensive representation of India's cultural tapestry. We evaluate SANSKRITI on leading Large Language Models (LLMs), Indic Language Models (ILMs), and Small Language Models (SLMs), revealing significant disparities in their ability to handle culturally nuanced queries, with many models struggling in region-specific contexts. By offering an extensive, culturally rich, and diverse dataset, SANSKRITI sets a new standard for assessing and improving the cultural understanding of LMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SANSKRITI, a new benchmark dataset for evaluating language models' (LMs) understanding of Indian culture. SANSKRITI consists of over 21,000 question-answer pairs covering 16 key cultural attributes across all 28 states and 8 union territories of India. The authors evaluate several Large Language Models (LLMs), Indic Language Models (ILMs), and Small Language Models (SLMs) on SANSKRITI and find significant performance variations, particularly in region-specific contexts. The paper argues that SANSKRITI fills a gap in existing benchmarks, which often overlook cultural depth in favor of multilingualism. The authors make the dataset publicly available to encourage research in culturally inclusive AI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing an Important Gap:** The paper directly addresses a critical gap in current LM evaluation: the lack of benchmarks assessing cultural understanding, particularly within the Indian context. This is significant because truly effective LMs need to operate well across diverse socio-cultural settings.
    *   **Comprehensive Cultural Coverage:**  SANSKRITI's scope is impressive.  Covering all Indian states and union territories across 16 attributes offers a granular view of cultural understanding that few datasets have attempted.
    *   **Public Availability:** The open release of the dataset is a significant contribution to the research community, enabling further investigation and improvements in culturally aware LMs.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The dataset creation process appears well-structured, with a dedicated team of annotators, clear guidelines, and quality assurance measures.
    *   **Baseline Evaluations:** Providing evaluations across a variety of model architectures (LLMs, ILMs, SLMs) gives a useful starting point for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Question Format Limitations:** The reliance on multiple-choice questions with fact-based answers limits the assessment of deeper reasoning or nuanced understanding of cultural concepts. This format might favor simple knowledge retrieval over true comprehension.
    *   **Limited Multilingual Representation:** The paper acknowledges the absence of state-specific multilingual queries. Given India's vast linguistic diversity, the lack of localized language considerations is a significant limitation. A benchmark that truly captures cultural understanding must also grapple with linguistic nuances.
    *   **Lack of Diversity in Question Types:**  The questions are predominantly fact-based, and the paper acknowledges a lack of questions that assess causal understanding. This limits the evaluation of the models' ability to reason about cultural phenomena.
    *   **The definition of parameters cutoff to be LLM, SLM and ILM is arbitrary.** This could have been better justified or explained with some ablation experiment.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's primary novelty lies in *creating and releasing* a benchmark focused on the cultural context of India. While other datasets exist for multilingualism or regional dialects, SANSKRITI's systematic focus on cultural attributes across all Indian states is a clear contribution.
    *   The *significance* depends on how the research community adopts the dataset.  If SANSKRITI becomes a standard for evaluating LMs in the Indian context and inspires further research into culturally-aware AI, its impact will be substantial.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper presents a valuable resource for the NLP community by addressing the crucial need for cultural awareness in LMs. However, the limitations in question format, multilingual coverage, and reasoning capabilities need to be acknowledged. The paper opens the door for many follow-up efforts to address these limitations.
    The dataset creation effort appears substantial and the benchmark evaluation is rigorous but the contribution still has limited novelty from a fundamental perspective.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The score reflects the significance of addressing a critical gap and providing a comprehensive dataset. The paper presents a meticulously created dataset that tackles an important, but often overlooked, dimension of LM evaluation. Its potential impact on fostering culturally aware AI research in the Indian context is significant.  However, limitations in question format, limited multilingual capabilities, and a lack of nuanced reasoning assessments prevent a higher score. This benchmark primarily tests memorization rather than true cultural understanding. It has incremental, but not transformative, novelty.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Sampling 3D Molecular Conformers with Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15378v1)
- **Authors**: J. Thorben Frank, Winfried Ripken, Gregor Lied, Klaus-Robert Müller, Oliver T. Unke, Stefan Chmiela
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) have demonstrated strong performance in generative modeling, particularly in image synthesis, making them a compelling choice for molecular conformer generation. However, applying DiTs to molecules introduces novel challenges, such as integrating discrete molecular graph information with continuous 3D geometry, handling Euclidean symmetries, and designing conditioning mechanisms that generalize across molecules of varying sizes and structures. We propose DiTMC, a framework that adapts DiTs to address these challenges through a modular architecture that separates the processing of 3D coordinates from conditioning on atomic connectivity. To this end, we introduce two complementary graph-based conditioning strategies that integrate seamlessly with the DiT architecture. These are combined with different attention mechanisms, including both standard non-equivariant and SO(3)-equivariant formulations, enabling flexible control over the trade-off between between accuracy and computational efficiency. Experiments on standard conformer generation benchmarks (GEOM-QM9, -DRUGS, -XL) demonstrate that DiTMC achieves state-of-the-art precision and physical validity. Our results highlight how architectural choices and symmetry priors affect sample quality and efficiency, suggesting promising directions for large-scale generative modeling of molecular structures. Code available at https://github.com/ML4MolSim/dit_mc.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces DiTMC, a Diffusion Transformer framework tailored for generating 3D molecular conformers.  It addresses challenges inherent in applying DiTs to molecules, such as integrating graph information with continuous 3D geometry, handling Euclidean symmetries (translation and rotation), and designing conditioning mechanisms that generalize across varying molecule sizes and structures. DiTMC uses a modular architecture separating coordinate processing from atomic connectivity conditioning. It presents two graph-based conditioning strategies integrated with the DiT architecture, combined with different attention mechanisms (non-equivariant and SO(3)-equivariant), to control accuracy and efficiency. Experiments on benchmark datasets (GEOM-QM9, DRUGS, XL) demonstrate state-of-the-art precision and physical validity. The code is made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates reasonable novelty by adapting the Diffusion Transformer architecture, which is well-established in image synthesis, to the molecular conformer generation problem.  While DiTs have been explored in other scientific domains, the specific adaptations for handling molecular graphs, Euclidean symmetries, and the design of effective conditioning mechanisms represent a non-trivial contribution. The modularity allowing systematic study of attention mechanisms is also a valuable feature. The introduction of two distinct graph conditioning strategies (bond-pair and all-pair) contributes to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** Molecular conformer generation is an important problem in computational chemistry and drug discovery. Achieving state-of-the-art results on standard benchmarks suggests that DiTMC could be a practically useful tool. The ablation studies examining the impact of different attention mechanisms (equivariant vs. non-equivariant) provide valuable insights for future research in this area. The demonstration of high physical validity, as evidenced by accurate prediction of ensemble properties, further strengthens the significance.  The code release facilitates reproducibility and wider adoption of the method. The exploration of scaling and generalization is a plus. However, while the results are strong on established benchmarks, there is little discussion on the practical impact or limitations of these conformer generation approaches in real-world discovery scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong empirical results on standard benchmarks.
    *   Detailed ablation studies investigating the impact of different architectural choices.
    *   Well-written and clearly explained methodology.
    *   Addresses key challenges in applying diffusion models to molecular data.
    *   Modular architecture facilitating further research.
    *   Good discussion of the trade-offs between accuracy and computational cost when using equivariant methods.
    *   Public code release.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The empirical evaluation, while thorough, primarily focuses on standard benchmarks. The paper would benefit from a discussion of performance on novel, more complex datasets, or in the context of a real-world drug discovery application.
    *   Although the paper emphasizes efficiency, the equivariant models are significantly slower. A deeper investigation into optimizing the equivariant implementation or exploring alternative approximations could be valuable.
    *   The limitations section, although present, could be more detailed, perhaps including issues of scalability to larger, more flexible molecules and the dependence on high-quality ground truth conformers for training.

* **Overall Impact:** The work has the potential to influence the development of generative models for molecular structures. The systematic exploration of design choices, particularly in handling symmetry, provides valuable guidance for future research. The modular architecture encourages experimentation with different components and conditioning strategies.

*Score: 7*

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by successfully adapting a Diffusion Transformer architecture to molecular conformer generation and achieving state-of-the-art results. It systematically addresses the challenges inherent in working with molecular data and provides valuable insights into the impact of different design choices. The open-source code is a significant asset. However, the lack of validation in real-world applications and the computational cost of equivariant models slightly limit the overall impact and justifies a score of 7, rather than a higher one.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### When Model Knowledge meets Diffusion Model: Diffusion-assisted Data-free Image Synthesis with Alignment of Domain and Class
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15381v1)
- **Authors**: Yujin Kim, Hyunsoo Kim, Hyunwoo J. Kim, Suhyun Kim
- **Abstract**: Open-source pre-trained models hold great potential for diverse applications, but their utility declines when their training data is unavailable. Data-Free Image Synthesis (DFIS) aims to generate images that approximate the learned data distribution of a pre-trained model without accessing the original data. However, existing DFIS meth ods produce samples that deviate from the training data distribution due to the lack of prior knowl edge about natural images. To overcome this limitation, we propose DDIS, the first Diffusion-assisted Data-free Image Synthesis method that leverages a text-to-image diffusion model as a powerful image prior, improving synthetic image quality. DDIS extracts knowledge about the learned distribution from the given model and uses it to guide the diffusion model, enabling the generation of images that accurately align with the training data distribution. To achieve this, we introduce Domain Alignment Guidance (DAG) that aligns the synthetic data domain with the training data domain during the diffusion sampling process. Furthermore, we optimize a single Class Alignment Token (CAT) embedding to effectively capture class-specific attributes in the training dataset. Experiments on PACS and Ima geNet demonstrate that DDIS outperforms prior DFIS methods by generating samples that better reflect the training data distribution, achieving SOTA performance in data-free applications.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and can provide a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary**

The paper "When Model Knowledge meets Diffusion Model: Diffusion-assisted Data-free Image Synthesis with Alignment of Domain and Class" introduces DDIS, a novel data-free image synthesis (DFIS) method that utilizes a text-to-image (T2I) diffusion model as a strong image prior. DDIS aims to generate images that approximate the learned data distribution of a pre-trained model without accessing the original training data. The core idea is to guide the diffusion model by extracting knowledge about the learned distribution from the pre-trained model. To achieve this, the paper proposes Domain Alignment Guidance (DAG) and Class Alignment Token (CAT). DAG aligns the synthetic data domain with the training data domain during the diffusion sampling process, while CAT is a single optimized embedding to capture class-specific attributes. Experiments on PACS and ImageNet demonstrate that DDIS outperforms prior DFIS methods in terms of image quality and alignment with the training data distribution, leading to state-of-the-art performance in data-free applications like knowledge distillation and pruning.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the clever integration of a pre-trained T2I diffusion model into the DFIS framework. Previous DFIS methods struggled with generating realistic and diverse images due to the lack of prior knowledge. Leveraging the strong image priors learned by T2I models is a significant step forward. The specific techniques DAG and CAT are also novel, even if they are inspired by existing concepts (batch norm statistics for domain alignment, textual inversion). DAG's use of BN statistics for domain alignment is a unique way to incorporate the learned distribution into the diffusion process. The CAT approach, while inspired by similar token optimization strategies, is tailored to the DFIS setting to capture class-specific details missed by simple class labels.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in machine learning: the lack of access to training data for pre-trained models. DFIS methods have the potential to unlock the utility of these models in various applications where data access is restricted. By improving the quality and fidelity of generated images, DDIS significantly enhances the applicability of DFIS in tasks such as knowledge distillation and model pruning. The experimental results demonstrate the tangible benefits of DDIS over existing DFIS techniques in improving performance on downstream tasks. The ability to perform DFIS across a range of domains beyond "photo" is also significant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Sound Methodology:** The DAG and CAT techniques are well-motivated and integrated into a coherent framework.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate significant improvements over existing DFIS methods on both PACS and ImageNet. Ablation studies provide insight into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the proposed techniques.
    *   **Addresses a Key Challenge:** Solves the practical problem of data accessibility when using pre-trained models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on T2I Models:** The approach depends on the availability and performance of pre-trained T2I diffusion models. While these models are becoming increasingly powerful, their inherent biases and limitations could affect the generated images.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions improved efficiency compared to previous DFIS methods, optimizing the CAT embedding and generating high-resolution images with diffusion models can still be computationally expensive, especially for large datasets. Although this is justified in the cost benefits compared to DeepInversion.
    *   **Sketch Domain Limitations:** The paper acknowledges limitations in the sketch domain, suggesting that the DAG may need to be more model-agnostic. This could be an area for further research.
    *   **Potential Ethical Concerns:** While the paper addresses it, generating synthetic data can raise ethical concerns regarding data privacy and potential misuse, and its impact in downstream data free tasks. This warrants ongoing discussion and responsible development.

*   **Potential Influence:** DDIS has the potential to influence future research in DFIS by demonstrating the effectiveness of leveraging pre-trained generative models. It could also inspire new techniques for aligning synthetic data with target distributions and capturing fine-grained class-specific information.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of integrating T2I diffusion models into DFIS, the significance of addressing data accessibility issues, the strong empirical results, and the potential influence on future research, but taking into account the noted weaknesses (reliance on T2I models, potential ethical concerns, Sketch limitations), I assign a score of **8**. While not a groundbreaking paradigm shift, it represents a significant advancement in the field of DFIS and provides a practical solution to a common problem in machine learning.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Provable Maximum Entropy Manifold Exploration via Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15385v1)
- **Authors**: Riccardo De Santi, Marin Vlastelica, Ya-Ping Hsieh, Zebang Shen, Niao He, Andreas Krause
- **Abstract**: Exploration is critical for solving real-world decision-making problems such as scientific discovery, where the objective is to generate truly novel designs rather than mimic existing data distributions. In this work, we address the challenge of leveraging the representational power of generative models for exploration without relying on explicit uncertainty quantification. We introduce a novel framework that casts exploration as entropy maximization over the approximate data manifold implicitly defined by a pre-trained diffusion model. Then, we present a novel principle for exploration based on density estimation, a problem well-known to be challenging in practice. To overcome this issue and render this method truly scalable, we leverage a fundamental connection between the entropy of the density induced by a diffusion model and its score function. Building on this, we develop an algorithm based on mirror descent that solves the exploration problem as sequential fine-tuning of a pre-trained diffusion model. We prove its convergence to the optimal exploratory diffusion model under realistic assumptions by leveraging recent understanding of mirror flows. Finally, we empirically evaluate our approach on both synthetic and high-dimensional text-to-image diffusion, demonstrating promising results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper tackles the problem of exploration with generative models, specifically diffusion models. It introduces a framework that casts exploration as entropy maximization over the approximate data manifold implicitly defined by a pre-trained diffusion model. To address the challenges of density estimation, the paper leverages a connection between the entropy and the score function of the diffusion model. It proposes an algorithm (S-MEME) based on mirror descent for sequential fine-tuning of a pre-trained diffusion model and provides convergence guarantees. The approach is evaluated on synthetic data and a high-dimensional text-to-image diffusion task.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its approach to exploration. Instead of relying on explicit uncertainty quantification (common in RL-based exploration), it uses the *representational power* of diffusion models and casts exploration as entropy maximization *over the manifold*. The connection it draws between entropy and the score function is also a significant technical contribution, allowing the algorithm to circumvent the computationally expensive density estimation.  The framework is a clever way to avoid explicit uncertainty calculations which can be problematic in high dimensional spaces. This approach is conceptually distinct from typical uncertainty-based exploration methods.

*   **Significance:**  Diffusion models are powerful generative models, but leveraging them for exploration is an important direction, especially in scientific discovery where novel designs are required.  The theoretical convergence guarantees, even under idealized conditions, lend credence to the method's potential. The experimental results, particularly on the text-to-image task, demonstrate the practical relevance of the approach. The extension to incorporate validity checks is also valuable, enabling guided data augmentation. However, the reliance on some assumptions weakens its practicality, which reduces the overall impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined theoretical framework with convergence analysis.
    *   Scalable algorithm (S-MEME) that builds on existing diffusion model infrastructure.
    *   Demonstrated empirical performance on a challenging text-to-image task.
    *   Avoids explicit uncertainty quantification.
    *   Offers a way to trade off exploration with validity/feasibility constraints.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The theoretical analysis relies on strong assumptions, such as exact score estimation and an idealized optimization oracle (Assumption 5.1).  While the later sections generalize to more realistic settings and leverage mirror flows, the initial convergence analysis is limited by these assumptions. These assumptions might not be easily met in practice, which can limit the applicability of the theoretical results.
    *   While the empirical results are promising, further analysis is needed. The proxy metrics used (FID and CLIP) only provide a partial view of the exploration process. More thorough evaluation of the generated samples concerning real-world utility/novelty is required.
    *   The computational cost of training diffusion models is significant. Although the proposed method is scalable in a sense that it avoids some costly calculations, the base cost of the diffusion models can still limit its widespread adoption.

*   **Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence the way exploration is done in generative models. It provides a concrete framework and algorithm, and a theoretical foundation that can be built upon. However, the reliance on potentially unrealistic assumptions and the computational cost of diffusion models will limit the immediate impact. More work is needed to address these limitations and make the method more practical and accessible.

*   **Score:** 7

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a novel framework for exploration, but has theoretical limitations that reduce its practical significance. The algorithmic principle is well-motivated and demonstrated empirically, but the strong assumptions on estimation and optimization impact the theoretical analysis. Considering the conceptual novelty, and the promise of using diffusion models for exploration, the paper warrants a score of 7. Further investigation to make the assumptions more realistic is desirable for the paper to gain more recognition within the scientific community.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Targeted Lexical Injection: Unlocking Latent Cross-Lingual Alignment in Lugha-Llama via Early-Layer LoRA Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15415v1)
- **Authors**: Stanley Ngugi
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities, yet their performance in low-resource languages (LRLs), such as Swahili, often lags due to data scarcity and underrepresentation in pre-training. A key challenge is achieving robust cross-lingual lexical alignment, crucial for tasks like translation and cross-lingual information retrieval. This paper introduces Targeted Lexical Injection (TLI), a novel and efficient fine-tuning approach. We first demonstrate that Lugha-Llama-8B-wura, a Swahili-centric LLM, exhibits strong, near-perfect lexical alignment for Swahili-English word pairs in its early internal layers (specifically Layer 2, with ~0.99998 average cosine similarity based on a pilot study), a capability not fully reflected in its final output representations (baseline ~0.32 similarity on our evaluation set). TLI leverages this insight by using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) and a contrastive learning objective to fine-tune the model, specifically targeting embeddings from this empirically identified optimal early layer. Our experiments show that TLI significantly improves the output-level lexical alignment for 623 trained Swahili-English word pairs, increasing average cosine similarity from 0.3211 to 0.4113 (+28.08%, p < 1.33 x 10^-240). More importantly, these improvements generalize remarkably well to 63 unseen control word pairs, with similarity increasing from 0.3143 to 0.4033 (+28.32%, p < 7.17 x 10^-27). These findings suggest TLI enhances the model's ability to preserve and propagate its inherent early-layer cross-lingual knowledge, offering a parameter-efficient and effective strategy for improving lexical alignment in LRL-focused LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Targeted Lexical Injection (TLI), a novel Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA)-based fine-tuning approach to improve cross-lingual lexical alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly focusing on low-resource languages (LRLs) like Swahili.  The core idea is to leverage and amplify existing but underutilized cross-lingual knowledge residing in early layers of the LLM. The authors empirically demonstrate that Lugha-Llama-8B-wura, a Swahili-centric LLM, possesses strong lexical alignment in its early layers (Layer 2) that is not reflected in its final output. TLI fine-tunes the model using LoRA and a contrastive learning objective, specifically targeting embeddings from this optimal early layer.  Experiments show significant improvements in output-level lexical alignment, which generalizes well to unseen Swahili-English word pairs. The paper argues that TLI improves the model's ability to preserve and propagate its inherent cross-lingual knowledge, offering a parameter-efficient and effective strategy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several ideas with a specific, targeted application. While LoRA and contrastive learning are not new, the insight to target a specific, empirically identified early layer with pre-existing strong cross-lingual alignment and the resulting generalization effect *is* a novel contribution. Most fine-tuning approaches tend to focus on output layer optimization or train all layers equally. TLI's focus on unlocking and amplifying existing knowledge within a specific layer is a fresh perspective.
* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential impact on improving LLM performance in LRLs. Data scarcity is a major bottleneck for LRLs, making data-efficient fine-tuning techniques like TLI highly valuable. The generalization results suggest that TLI isn't just memorizing specific pairs but improving the model's underlying cross-lingual understanding. If the approach can be generalized to other LRLs and models, it could have a significant impact on making LLMs more equitable and accessible for diverse languages. Moreover, finding that initial layers align on the translation before other layers are trained opens up a lot of possibilities for LLM manipulation and interpretation.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenge of cross-lingual lexical alignment in LRLs and motivates the need for improved techniques.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The study provides strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of TLI through both quantitative and qualitative analysis. The statistical significance of the results is compelling.
    * **Generalization Results:** The robust generalization to unseen word pairs is a significant strength, indicating that TLI is learning something fundamental rather than just memorizing.
    * **Pilot Study Layer Selection:** The early-layer selection strategy using the pilot study adds rigor to the methodology and provides valuable insights into the model's internal workings.
    * **Well-written and Organized:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making it easy to understand the methodology and results.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The study is limited to Lugha-Llama-8B-wura and the Swahili-English language pair. While the authors argue for the generalizability of the approach, more evidence is needed across different models and languages. The model could perform differently on other languages.
    * **Dataset Size:** The training and control sets, although carefully curated, are of finite size. Larger datasets could provide more robust validation of the results.
    * **Lack of Downstream Task Evaluation:** The paper focuses primarily on lexical alignment. Evaluating the impact of TLI on downstream tasks such as machine translation or cross-lingual information retrieval would strengthen the argument for its practical value.  It provides no external measurements for the performance.
    * **Limited Interpretability:** While the paper hypothesizes about improved information flow, it lacks a deep dive into the precise neural mechanisms modified by LoRA to achieve the observed improvements. The exact changes and how they improve cross-lingual embedding is only speculated.
    * **Layer Universality:** The paper assumes Layer 2 is a universal location for the injection. A more robust method for locating the cross-lingual data would strengthen the argument.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to improve cross-lingual lexical alignment in LLMs for LRLs. The empirical results are compelling, particularly the generalization effect. The method is likely less powerful than other approaches, because it only works with what the base model "already knows" - but is a good place to start if you are having trouble with a specific pair of translations. It makes a valuable contribution to the field by offering a data-efficient and parameter-efficient pathway to enhance cross-lingual understanding. The limitations, mainly related to scope and downstream evaluation, are acknowledged and provide directions for future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Understanding GUI Agent Localization Biases through Logit Sharpness
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15425v1)
- **Authors**: Xingjian Tao, Yiwei Wang, Yujun Cai, Zhicheng Yang, Jing Tang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have enabled GUI agents to interact with operating systems by grounding language into spatial actions. Despite their promising performance, these models frequently exhibit hallucinations-systematic localization errors that compromise reliability. We propose a fine-grained evaluation framework that categorizes model predictions into four distinct types, revealing nuanced failure modes beyond traditional accuracy metrics. To better quantify model uncertainty, we introduce the Peak Sharpness Score (PSS), a metric that evaluates the alignment between semantic continuity and logits distribution in coordinate prediction. Building on this insight, we further propose Context-Aware Cropping, a training-free technique that improves model performance by adaptively refining input context. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our framework and methods provide actionable insights and enhance the interpretability and robustness of GUI agent behavior.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper tackles the problem of localization errors (hallucinations) in GUI agents powered by Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It proposes a fine-grained evaluation framework to categorize these errors into correct responses, biased hallucinations, misleading hallucinations, and confusion hallucinations.  To quantify model uncertainty, the authors introduce the Peak Sharpness Score (PSS), a metric evaluating the alignment between semantic continuity and logit distribution during coordinate prediction.  Finally, they propose a training-free Context-Aware Cropping technique to refine the input context and improve performance.  Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the evaluation framework and the cropping method in providing insights and enhancing GUI agent behavior.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates moderate novelty. While prior work has focused on improving the accuracy of GUI agents, the paper's novelty lies primarily in its systematic analysis and categorization of localization errors and its introduction of the Peak Sharpness Score (PSS). The Context-Aware Cropping technique, while effective, is not entirely novel, as image cropping techniques are common in computer vision. However, the application of this technique in a GUI agent context, and its specific design to address localization bias makes it a useful contribution.

* **Significance:** The significance of the paper is in its shift toward a more nuanced understanding of GUI agent errors. The proposed evaluation framework allows researchers to move beyond simple accuracy metrics and gain deeper insights into the types of errors models are making. The PSS provides a quantifiable measure of model confidence, which can be useful for debugging and improving models. While the Context-Aware Cropping method offers a practical way to improve performance, its primary significance is in its contribution toward identifying and mitigating specific types of model error.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly defines the problem of localization errors and motivates the need for a more fine-grained analysis.
    * **Systematic Approach:** The paper provides a systematic framework for categorizing hallucination types and analyzing the root causes of errors.
    * **Useful Metric:** The PSS is a well-defined and potentially useful metric for evaluating model confidence and identifying uncertainty.
    * **Effective Technique:** The Context-Aware Cropping technique effectively improves model performance, especially in cases of biased hallucinations.
    * **Reproducible Experiments:**  The experimental setup is well-described, and the results are presented clearly.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty of Cropping:** The cropping technique, while effective, is not fundamentally novel. The novelty lies in its application.
    * **Context-Aware Cropping relies on Predicted Coordinates:** It is important to acknowledge that relying on the coordinates predicted by a hallucinating model may not always improve performance. In cases of confusion hallucinations, for example, the cropping technique may cause the model to hallucinate even further by zooming in on a completely irrelevant region of the image.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and reliable GUI agents by providing a more detailed understanding of model errors and offering a practical technique for improving performance. The PSS could become a standard metric for evaluating model confidence in GUI agent tasks.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents valuable contributions in the form of a systematic error analysis and a useful metric (PSS) for quantifying model confidence. The Context-Aware Cropping technique is a practical solution for improving performance, though its novelty is limited. The paper fills a gap in understanding the nuances of GUI agent errors, paving the way for more robust and reliable models. The limitations of context-aware cropping reduce the score from a higher ranking, but the paper does demonstrate significant insight into the problem domain that deserves high recognition.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Uncovering Intention through LLM-Driven Code Snippet Description Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15453v1)
- **Authors**: Yusuf Sulistyo Nugroho, Farah Danisha Salam, Brittany Reid, Raula Gaikovina Kula, Kazumasa Shimari, Kenichi Matsumoto
- **Abstract**: Documenting code snippets is essential to pinpoint key areas where both developers and users should pay attention. Examples include usage examples and other Application Programming Interfaces (APIs), which are especially important for third-party libraries. With the rise of Large Language Models (LLMs), the key goal is to investigate the kinds of description developers commonly use and evaluate how well an LLM, in this case Llama, can support description generation. We use NPM Code Snippets, consisting of 185,412 packages with 1,024,579 code snippets. From there, we use 400 code snippets (and their descriptions) as samples. First, our manual classification found that the majority of original descriptions (55.5%) highlight example-based usage.
